<p>Widely used editions of Chrysostom's works are available in Greek, Latin, English, and French. The Greek edition is edited by <a href="/wiki/Sir_Henry_Savile" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Henry Savile">Sir Henry Savile</a> (eight volumes, Eton, 1613); the most complete Greek and Latin edition is edited by Bernard de Montfaucon (thirteen volumes, Paris, 1718–38, republished in 1834–40, and reprinted in Migne's <i>Patrologia Graeca</i>, volumes 47–64). There is an English translation in the first series of the <i>Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers</i> (London and New York, 1889–90). A selection of his writings has been published more recently in the original with facing French translation in <i><a href="/wiki/Sources_Chr%C3%A9tiennes" title="Sources Chrétiennes">Sources Chrétiennes</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.cecs.acu.edu.au/chrysostombibliography.html">Cecs.acu.edu.au</a> hosts an online bibliography of scholarship on John Chrysostom.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Chrysostom&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Chrysostom&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Chrysostom&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The exact date of John's birth is in question, and dates between 344 and 349 are given. In the most recent general biography of Chrysostom, eminent patristics scholar <a href="/wiki/JND_Kelly" class="mw-redirect" title="JND Kelly">JND Kelly</a>, after a review of the evidence and literature, favours 349 as the date that best fits all available evidence, in agreement with Robert Carter. See Kelly, <i>Golden Mouth: (He was named Chrysosyom because that means Golder mouth) The Story of John Chrysostom: Ascetic, Preacher, Bishop</i>, (Grand Rapids: Baker Academic 1998: originally published Ithaca: Cornell University Press, 1995), p. 4 fn. 12; esp. Appendix B <i>passim</i>. For a discussion of alternatives presented in the literature, see Robert Carter, "The Chronology of St. John Chrysostom's Early Life", in <i>Traditio</i> 18:357–64 (1962); Jean Dumortier, "La valeur historique du dialogue de Palladius et la chronologie de saint Jean Chrysostome", in <i>Mélanges de science religieuse</i>, 8:51–56 (1951). Carter dates his birth to the year 349. See also <a href="#CITEREFWilken2004">Wilken 2004</a>, p.&#160;5</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-18"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-18">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFWilken2004">Wilken 2004</a>, p.&#160;7 prefers 368 for the date of Chrysostom's baptism, the <i>Encyclopaedia Judaica</i> prefers the later date of 373.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-81"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-81">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">A variant relates that this was <a href="/wiki/Genevieve_of_Brabant" title="Genevieve of Brabant">Genevieve of Brabant</a>, wife of Count Siegfried of <a href="/wiki/Treves" class="mw-redirect" title="Treves">Treves</a>, who was unjustly accused of infidelity and sentenced to death. She was led into the forest to be put to death, but her executioners relented and there abandoned her.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Citations">Citations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Chrysostom&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Citations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<li id="cite_note-episcopal-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-episcopal_5-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-episcopal_5-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.episcopalchurch.org/glossary/chrysostom-john/">"Chrysostom, John", <i>An Episcopal Dictionary of the Church</i>, The Episcopal Church</a></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-6"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-6">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The exact date of John's birth is in question, and dates between 344 and 349 are often given, and limits set at 340 and 350 (Kelly 296). In the most recent general biography of Chrysostom, eminent patristics scholar <a href="/wiki/JND_Kelly" class="mw-redirect" title="JND Kelly">JND Kelly</a>, after a review of the evidence and literature, favours 349 as the date that best fits all available evidence, in agreement with Robert Carter. See Kelly, <i>Golden Mouth: The Story of John Chrysostom: Ascetic, Preacher, Bishop</i>, Grand Rapids, MI: Baker Academic, 1998: originally published Ithaca: Cornell University Press, 1995), p. 4 fn. 12; esp. 296–298 <i>passim</i>. For a concurring analysis which is followed in most recent reconstructions of the early life of Chrysostomos, see Robert Carter, "The Chronology of St. John Chrysostom's Early Life", in <i>Traditio</i> 18:357–364 (1962). For a discussion of alternatives, often in older literature, see especially G. Ettlinger, <i>Traditio</i> 16 (1960), pp. 373–380, Jean Dumortier, "La valeur historique du dialogue de Palladius et la chronologie de saint Jean Chrysostome", <i>Mélanges de science religieuse</i>, 8:51–56 (1951)</span>
</li>

</li>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Works">Works</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Chrysostom&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Works">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/search.php?query=%28%28subject%3A%22Chrysostom%2C%20John%22%20OR%20subject%3A%22John%20Chrysostom%22%20OR%20creator%3A%22Chrysostom%2C%20John%22%20OR%20creator%3A%22John%20Chrysostom%22%20OR%20creator%3A%22Chrysostom%2C%20J%2E%22%20OR%20title%3A%22John%20Chrysostom%22%20OR%20description%3A%22Chrysostom%2C%20John%22%20OR%20description%3A%22John%20Chrysostom%22%29%20OR%20%28%22349-407%22%20AND%20Chrysostom%29%29%20AND%20%28-mediatype:software%29">Works by or about John Chrysostom</a> at <a href="/wiki/Internet_Archive" title="Internet Archive">Internet Archive</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://librivox.org/author/2129">Works by John Chrysostom</a> at <a href="/wiki/LibriVox" title="LibriVox">LibriVox</a> (public domain audiobooks) <img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Speaker_Icon.svg/15px-Speaker_Icon.svg.png" decoding="async" width="15" height="15" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Speaker_Icon.svg/23px-Speaker_Icon.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Speaker_Icon.svg/30px-Speaker_Icon.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="500" /></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.orthodoxyork.org/liturgy.html">The Divine Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.byzcath.org/forums/ubbthreads.php/ubb/download/Number/1617/filename/Study-Liturgicon-Chrysostom.pdf">Study Text of the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom (Ruthenian Edition, with Scriptural references)</a></li>
<li>Writings of Chrysostom in the <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://ccel.org">Christian Classics Ethereal Library</a> edition of the <i>Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers:</i>
<ul><li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20060925044220/http://www.ccel.org/fathers2/NPNF1-09/TOC.htm">On the Priesthood, Ascetic Treatises, Select Homilies and Letters, Homilies on the Statues</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20060925044203/http://www.ccel.org/fathers2/NPNF1-10/TOC.htm">Homilies on the Gospel of St. Matthew</a> '</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20060925044103/http://www.ccel.org/fathers2/NPNF1-11/TOC.htm">Homilies on the Acts of the Apostles and the Epistle to the Romans</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20060925044022/http://www.ccel.org/fathers2/NPNF1-12/TOC.htm">Homilies on First and Second Corinthians</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20060925044351/http://www.ccel.org/fathers2/NPNF1-13/TOC.htm">Homilies on the Epistles to the Galatians, Ephesians, Philippians, Colossians, Thessalonians, Timothy, Titus, and Philemon</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20060925043512/http://www.ccel.org/fathers2/NPNF1-14/TOC.htm">Homilies on the Gospel of St. John and the Epistle to the Hebrews</a></li></ul></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.christopherklitou.com/the_easter_sermon_chrysostom_hieratikon.htm">The <i>Hieratikon</i></a> Easter Sermon of St. John Chrysostom</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.fordham.edu/halsall/source/chrysostom-jews6.html">Eight Homilies Against the Jews</a></li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.documentacatholicaomnia.eu/20_30_0345-0407-_Iohannes_Chrysostomus,_Sanctus.html"><i>Opera Omnia</i></a> by Migne <i><a href="/wiki/Patrologia_Graeca" title="Patrologia Graeca">Patrologia Graeca</a></i> with analytical indexes</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://web.wlu.ca/history/cnighman/page12.html">The Auxiliary Resources page on the Electronic Manipulus florum Project Website</a> provides digital transcriptions of the Latin translations of <i>De laudibus sancti Pauli homeliae</i> (PG 50, 473–514), <i>Dialogus de sacerdotio</i> (PG 48, 623–91), and <i>In epistolam ad Hebraeos homeliae</i> (PG 63, 9–236), as well as the Latin text of the Pseudo-Chrysostom <i><a href="/wiki/Opus_Imperfectum" title="Opus Imperfectum">Opus imperfectum in Mattheum</a></i> (PG 56, 611–946). It also provides digital transcriptions of Anianus of Celeda's prologue on the homilies on Matthew and his Latin translations of the first eight homilies (PG 58, 975–1058) and also Anianus of Celeda's prologue and his Latin translations of Chrysostom's homelies 1–25 on Matthew from the <i>editio princeps</i> published in Venice in 1503.</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://clioproject.net">The <i>Chrysostomus Latinus in Iohannem</i> Online (CLIO) Project</a> is an Open Access resource that provides Burgundio of Pisa's translation of Chrysostom's 88 homilies on the Gospel of John (1173), which has never been printed, as well as the later Latin translations of Francesco Griffolini (1462) and Bernard de Montfaucon (1728), along with Montfaucon's critical edition of the original Greek text.</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://web.wlu.ca/history/cnighman/CLIMO/index.html">The <i>Chrysostomus Latinus in Mattheum</i> Online (CLIMO) Project</a> is a new Open Access project that seeks to follow the successful format of the CLIO Project. At present (July 2020) it provides transcriptions of Burgundio's preface and Homily 2.</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.tertullian.org/fathers/chrysostom_homily_2_on_christmas.htm">Ps.Chrysostom Homily 2 on Christmas</a> at Tertullian.org and <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/JohnChrysostomHomily2OnTheNativityOfOurSaviorJesusChrist">here</a> at Archive.org.</li>
<li><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://openn.library.upenn.edu/Data/0012/html/lcp_ms1.html">S. John Chrysostom: Homilies at OPenn</a></li></ul>
